{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WPvzw9aV54jUgxS96TPwbb","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Carbon Health Technologies, Inc. — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://carbonhealth.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Carbon Health Technologies, Inc. is a venture-backed healthcare technology company and clinical care provider founded in 2015 in San Francisco by Udemy co-founder Eren Bali, engineer Tom Berry, and physician Greg Burell. In 2018, Carbon Health merged with Direct Urgent Care (founded by emergency medicine physician Caesar Djavaherian), combining its technology platform with a physical clinic network. The company raised over $600 million across multiple funding rounds, including a $350M Series C led by Blackstone in 2021, reaching a peak valuation of approximately $3.3 billion. CVS Health led a $100M investment in 2023 to pilot clinics within CVS retail stores.\n     9→\n    10→Carbon Health operates as both a healthcare provider (urgent care and primary care clinics) and a technology company. It built a proprietary EHR platform called CarbyOS, initially for use in its own clinics. In late 2022, the company announced plans to license CarbyOS to external healthcare providers. As of its most recent disclosures, CarbyOS claims to serve over 1 million patients, 1,200 providers, and 200 locations — suggesting meaningful external adoption beyond Carbon Health's own ~93 clinics.\n    11→\n    12→In August 2024, CEO Eren Bali stepped down to return to Udemy, and COO Kerem Ozkay became CEO. In February 2026, Carbon Health filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy with liabilities exceeding $100 million, citing $100M–$500M in debt. The company secured $19.5M in debtor-in-possession financing and is pursuing a dual-track restructuring (debt-for-equity exchange or asset sale). The company states operations will continue without interruption during restructuring, including patient access to medical records.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: CarbyOs EHR\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 11590 (version 2), 11700 (version 3)\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→CarbyOS is described as an \"AI-powered operating system for care delivery.\" It is a cloud-based, proprietary EHR platform originally built by Carbon Health for its own urgent care and primary care clinics, now also licensed to external healthcare organizations. The platform integrates clinical documentation, billing/RCM, operations management, and patient engagement into a unified system.\n    21→\n    22→The CHPL certification covers two versions:\n    23→- **Version 2** (CHPL 11590, certified January 2025): Minimal certification — only (b)(10) EHI export plus infrastructure/security criteria (d)(1), (d)(5), (d)(6), (d)(8), (d)(9), (d)(12), (d)(13), (g)(4), (g)(5). This appears to be the initial certification for export compliance.\n    24→- **Version 3** (CHPL 11700, certified September 2025): Broad certification covering 30+ criteria including clinical data capture (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1), clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3), patient access (e)(3), FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10), public health (h)(1), and additional security/infrastructure criteria. This represents the full-featured certified product.\n    25→\n    26→Both versions represent the same platform (CarbyOS EHR), with version 3 being a more broadly certified iteration.\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→**Primary users**: Physicians, nurse practitioners, and clinical staff in urgent care and primary care settings. Also billing/coding staff and practice operations managers.\n    31→\n    32→**Clinical settings**: Primarily ambulatory — urgent care clinics, primary care offices, virtual care. Carbon Health's own network includes ~93 clinics across eight states (California, Colorado, Kansas, Massachusetts, New Jersey, Texas, Washington, and others). The company also piloted clinics inside CVS retail stores.\n    33→\n    34→**Customer scale**: CarbyOS reports serving 1,200+ providers and 200+ locations. At its peak, Carbon Health itself operated more clinics, but recent reporting in the bankruptcy filing lists approximately 93 clinics and 1,400 employees with ~480 providers. The gap between these numbers suggests meaningful external CarbyOS licensing has occurred.\n    35→\n    36→**End users**: Clinicians (documentation, orders, prescribing), billing staff (RCM, claims), operations managers (analytics, reporting), and patients (via mobile app for scheduling, records access, messaging).\n    37→\n    38→### Modules & Functionality\n    39→\n    40→CarbyOS is marketed with four core modules (per carbyos.com):\n    41→\n    42→1. **Care (EHR)**: The core clinical module. Described as \"provider-tested and approved\" with features including:\n    43→   - **AI-powered hands-free charting**: GPT-4-based ambient documentation that records doctor-patient conversations and generates SOAP notes. Carbon Health claims this was \"the first [EHR] to deploy native AI-assisted charting at scale\" (BusinessWire, June 2023). Reports indicate ~800,000+ AI-powered charts created, with 90%+ provider adoption.\n    44→   - **Template-based charting** with customizable templates and macros\n    45→   - **Clinical decision support** including AI-driven clinical suggestions\n    46→   - **E-prescribing** (consistent with (a)(14) certification for implantable device list, though e-prescribing itself is referenced in vendor materials and third-party reviews)\n    47→   - **Lab ordering and results viewing** (referenced in vendor blog and third-party sources)\n    48→   - **Medical imaging** (referenced in GetApp/third-party review listings)\n    49→   - **Referral management** with secure medical record sharing during referrals\n    50→   - **Care plans** with patient summaries and takeaways\n    51→   - **One-touch check-in and triage workflows**\n    52→   - **Automated ICD-10 and CPT coding suggestions** driven by AI during documentation\n    53→\n    54→2. **Health (Patient App)**: Mobile patient engagement application (iOS and Android) providing:\n    55→   - Same-day appointment scheduling\n    56→   - Video/virtual visits\n    57→   - Live doctor chat messaging\n    58→   - Treatment plan and medical record viewing\n    59→   - Lab results viewing\n    60→   - Prescription order tracking\n    61→   - Online billing and payment\n    62→   - \"Health Pass\" digital vaccine card (introduced during COVID-19 era)\n    63→\n    64→3. **Operate (Operations Platform)**: Analytics and operational insights for practice management, described as providing \"insights for business efficiency.\" Specific details are thin in public materials.\n    65→\n    66→4. **Billing (Intelligent RCM)**: A fully integrated revenue cycle management module (per the Carby Billing blog post). Features include:\n    67→   - Real-time eligibility (RTE) verification at check-in\n    68→   - Automated calculation of patient responsibility (co-pays, co-insurance, deductibles, out-of-pocket limits)\n    69→   - Credit card payment processing (claims 98%+ same-day patient payment capture)\n    70→   - Ambient AI analysis suggesting coding during visits\n    71→   - Automated extraction of diagnostic/procedural data for coding\n    72→   - Claims scrubbing via customizable rules engine\n    73→   - Automated identification/correction of missing or invalid codes\n    74→   - Batch claim correction\n    75→   - Full claims lifecycle visibility\n    76→   - Built-in safeguards requiring complete coding and care plans before chart sign-off\n    77→\n    78→**Additional capabilities** referenced in certification and vendor materials:\n    79→- **Transitions of Care / C-CDA exchange** (certified for (b)(1))\n    80→- **Clinical Quality Measures** (certified for (c)(1)–(c)(3), with CQM 122v13 listed)\n    81→- **FHIR API access** (certified for (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10) — FHIR v1.2 documentation available per disclosures page)\n    82→- **Public health reporting — immunization registry** (certified for (h)(1))\n    83→- **EHI Export** as C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for billing/claims information (per mandatory disclosures page)\n    84→- **DataMotion** listed as dependent software (likely for Direct messaging / secure health information exchange)\n    85→- **SAML 2.0 SSO** integration with identity providers (Okta, Microsoft Entra)\n    86→\n    87→### Data & Content\n    88→\n    89→Based on the vendor materials, certifications, and feature descriptions, CarbyOS stores and manages the following categories of data:\n    90→\n    91→**Clinical Data** (supported by (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14) certifications and vendor descriptions):\n    92→- Patient demographics and history\n    93→- SOAP notes / clinical documentation (including AI-generated visit summaries)\n    94→- Problem lists, medication lists, medication allergy lists\n    95→- Vital signs, lab results, diagnostic imaging references\n    96→- Care plans and patient instructions\n    97→- Clinical decision support alerts\n    98→- Implantable device list\n    99→- Family health history\n   100→\n   101→**Orders and Prescribing**:\n   102→- E-prescriptions (referenced in multiple sources)\n   103→- Lab orders and results\n   104→- Imaging orders\n   105→- Referral packages with associated clinical records\n   106→\n   107→**Billing and Financial Data** (from the Carby Billing module):\n   108→- Insurance eligibility and benefit information\n   109→- Claims data (ICD-10, CPT codes)\n   110→- Patient financial responsibility calculations\n   111→- Payment records (credit card processing)\n   112→- Billing workflow status and audit trails\n   113→- Claim scrubbing rules and correction history\n   114→\n   115→**Patient Engagement Data** (from the patient app and portal):\n   116→- Appointment scheduling records\n   117→- Patient-provider messages (chat and video visit records)\n   118→- Patient-accessible health records and treatment plans\n   119→- Prescription tracking information\n   120→- Vaccination records (Health Pass)\n   121→\n   122→**Transitions of Care / Interoperability**:\n   123→- C-CDA documents for clinical information exchange\n   124→- FHIR resources (per (g)(10) API certification)\n   125→- Immunization registry submissions (per (h)(1) certification)\n   126→- Direct messaging via DataMotion\n   127→\n   128→**Operational/Analytics Data** (from the Operate module):\n   129→- Operational performance metrics (details thin in public materials)\n   130→- Provider productivity data (e.g., keystrokes saved, charts created)\n   131→\n   132→**Notable**: The mandatory disclosures page explicitly states that EHI export includes \"C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for billing and claims information\" and excludes psychotherapy notes per privacy regulations. This confirms both clinical and billing data are within the export scope.\n   133→\n   134→**Gaps in research**: The Operate (analytics/operations) module has minimal public documentation about what data it specifically stores. Virtual visit recordings/transcripts are not explicitly discussed in terms of storage/retention, though the AI charting feature clearly processes audio recordings of visits. It's unclear whether the raw audio/transcripts are retained or only the generated notes.\n   135→\n   136→---\n   137→\n   138→## Business Context\n   139→\n   140→- **Cloud-hosted**: CarbyOS is a cloud-based platform, consistent with Carbon Health's technology-forward positioning.\n   141→- **Bankruptcy impact**: The February 2026 Chapter 11 filing introduces uncertainty about the product's long-term viability. The dual-track process (restructuring or asset sale) could result in CarbyOS being acquired by another entity. However, the company states operations continue during restructuring.\n   142→- **Product evolution**: CarbyOS evolved from an internal tool to a commercially licensed product. The 2022 announcement of licensing plans and the subsequent ONC certifications (2025) represent a deliberate productization effort.\n   143→- **AI differentiation**: The heavy emphasis on AI-powered charting (GPT-4 based ambient documentation) is the product's primary market differentiator. Carbon Health claims this reduces charting time by 75% and saves ~1,200 keystrokes per visit.\n   144→- **No third-party reviews found**: CarbyOS does not appear to have a meaningful presence on G2, Capterra, or similar review platforms, likely reflecting its relatively recent entry as an externally-sold product and its origin as an internal tool.\n   145→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Certification Disclosures ONC Health IT Certification \n This Health IT Module is compliant with the ONC Certification Criteria for Health IT and has been certified by an ONC-ACB in accordance with the applicable certification criteria adopted by the Secretary of Health and Human Services. This certification does not represent an endorsement by the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services. \n Vendor Name: Carbon Health Technologies, Inc. \n Website: www.carbonhealth.com (opens in a new tab) \n Address: 2100 Franklin St., Suite 355, Oakland, CA, 94612 \n Contact: Haritha Atluri,Director of Clinical Informatics, haritha@carbonhealth.com , (707) 653-6724 \n Product Version: CarbyOS EHR 3 \n \n Date the product was certified – 9/25/25 \n Unique certification number - 15.04.04.3216.Carb.02.01.1.250925 \n Certification criteria to which the product has been certified - 170.315 (a)(1-3, 5, 12, 14); (b)(1, 10-11); (c)(1-3); (d)(1-9, 12-13); (e)(3); (g)(3-7, 9-10); (h)(1) \n CQMs to which the product has been certified - 122v13 \n Any additional software the certified product relied upon to demonstrate its compliance with certification criteria - DataMotion \n \n Multi-Factor Authentication \n Supported Use Cases for Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) \n CarbyOs EHR provides multi-factor authentication (MFA) in compliance with the Office of the National Coordinator for Health IT’s (ONC) Multi-Factor Authentication criterion at 45 CFR 170.315(d)(13). The following workflows support MFA within CarbyOs’s product offerings: \n \n Login to CarbyOs EHR \n \n Encompasses access for clinical, administrative, and support users. \n MFA is required for initial login and can be configured per organization’s security preferences. \n \n \n CarbyOs Mobile Access \n \n MFA requirements extend to users logging into CarbyOs EHR via mobile devices for secure, on-the-go access. \n \n \n \n MFA Options Available \n \n Built-in 2FA Verification: CarbyOs includes built-in MFA capabilities using SMS and email-based two-factor authentication (2FA) at no additional cost, ensuring all user logins are securely verified. \n Customer-Configured Identity Provider (IdP) Support: Organizations can enforce MFA using their own IdP through SAML 2.0 single sign-on (SSO) integration, including providers such as Okta and Microsoft Entra. Our engineering team is available to assist with custom IdP setups. \n \n This robust MFA support enhances the security of CarbyOs EHR, providing flexible, industry-standard authentication options for all users and workflow settings. \n EHI Export Functionality \n CarbyOs EHR meets the certification criterion §170.315(b)(10) for Electronic Health Information (EHI) export by enabling users to export EHI in an electronic and computable format. The EHI Export functionality supports both single-patient and patient population exports. \n \n \n Single-Patient EHI Export \n \n Users can export all EHI for an individual patient at any time without requiring developer assistance. \n Export formats include C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for billing and claims information, and original native formats for uploaded documents. \n The export is electronic, computable, ensuring accessibility without preconditions. \n \n \n \n Patient Population EHI Export \n \n Users can export all EHI for their entire patient population in bulk. \n The export is electronic, computable, and supports system migration or data transfer to other health IT products. \n \n \n \n Limitations and Exclusions \n \n EHI exports exclude psychotherapy notes as defined in 45 CFR 164.501 and information compiled for legal proceedings. \n System administrators manage the ability to perform EHI exports to maintain data security. \n \n \n \n Benefits of EHI Export in CarbyOs EHR \n \n Facilitates patient access to their records in a timely and computable manner. \n Supports healthcare organizations in transitioning to other health IT systems or performing bulk data migrations. \n Aligns with the interoperability and data access goals of the 21st Century Cures Act, ensuring compliance with federal requirements and enabling standardized data exchange. \n \n \n \n FHIR API Documentation \n CarbyOs EHR meets the certification criterion §170.315(g)(10) for Standardized API for patient and population services.\nThe API makes health information broadly available using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the HL7 standards organization.\nThis document describes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with FHIR-based data systems. \n Version Last Update Download v1.2 2025-05-30 Download \n Safety Enhanced Design Document \n Report based on The following workflows support MFA within CarbyOs’s product offerings: \n \n Login to CarbyOs EHR \n \n Encompasses access for clinical, administrative, and support users. \n MFA is required for initial login and can be configured per organization’s security preferences. \n \n \n CarbyOs Mobile Access \n \n MFA requirements extend to users logging into CarbyOs EHR via mobile devices for secure, on-the-go access. \n \n \n \n MFA Options Available \n \n Built-in 2FA Verification: CarbyOs includes built-in MFA capabilities using SMS and email-based two-factor authentication (2FA) at no additional cost, ensuring all user logins are securely verified. \n Customer-Configured Identity Provider (IdP) Support: Organizations can enforce MFA using their own IdP through SAML 2.0 single sign-on (SSO) integration, including providers such as Okta and Microsoft Entra. Our engineering team is available to assist with custom IdP setups. \n \n This robust MFA support enhances the security of CarbyOs EHR, providing flexible, industry-standard authentication options for all users and workflow settings. \n EHI Export Functionality \n CarbyOs EHR meets the certification criterion §170.315(b)(10) for Electronic Health Information (EHI) export by enabling users to export EHI in an electronic and computable format. The EHI Export functionality supports both single-patient and patient population exports. \n \n \n Single-Patient EHI Export \n \n Users can export all EHI for an individual patient at any time without requiring developer assistance. \n Export formats include C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for billing and claims information, and original native formats for uploaded documents. \n The export is electronic, computable, ensuring accessibility without preconditions. \n \n \n \n Patient Population EHI Export \n \n Users can export all EHI for their entire patient population in bulk. \n The export is electronic, computable, and supports system migration or data transfer to other health IT products. \n \n \n \n Limitations and Exclusions \n \n EHI exports exclude psychotherapy notes as defined in 45 CFR 164.501 and information compiled for legal proceedings. \n System administrators manage the ability to perform EHI exports to maintain data security. \n \n \n \n Benefits of EHI Export in CarbyOs EHR \n \n Facilitates patient access to their records in a timely and computable manner. \n Supports healthcare organizations in transitioning to other health IT systems or performing bulk data migrations. \n Aligns with the interoperability and data access goals of the 21st Century Cures Act, ensuring compliance with federal requirements and enabling standardized data exchange. \n \n \n \n FHIR API Documentation \n CarbyOs EHR meets the certification criterion §170.315(g)(10) for Standardized API for patient and population services.\nThe API makes health information broadly available using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the HL7 standards organization.\nThis document describes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with FHIR-based data systems. \n Version Last Update Download v1.2 2025-05-30 Download \n Safety Enhanced Design Document \n Report based on The API makes health information broadly\navailable using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the\nHL7 standards organization. FHIR is based on common web standards that can be reached\nthrough a RESTful protocol in which each FHIR resource has a known URL. This document\ndescribes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with\nFHIR-based data systems.\nWe follow Smart-On-FHIR authentication (OAuth2) and authorization (Ref.\nhttp://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/toc.html )\n\n3.​FHIR Endpoints\nNote: Production and Test Server endpoints are subject to change.\n\nProduction Endpoint\nFHIR Base Url :\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.production.awscarbonhealth.com/hapi-fhir\n\nAuthentication Server Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.production.awscarbonhealth.com/fhir-smart-auth\n\nTest Server Endpoint\nFHIR Base Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.alpha.awscarbonhealth.com/hapi-fhir\n\nAuthentication Server Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.alpha.awscarbonhealth.com/fhir-smart-auth\n\n6\n\n\f4.​Types of Smart-On-FHIR Applications\n●​ Standalone App: SMART on FHIR confidential client with a patient context, refresh\ntoken, and OpenID Connect (OIDC) identity token.\n●​ EHR Embedded app: Demonstrate the ability to perform an EHR launch to a\nSMART on FHIR\n●​ Smart Backend Services App (Multi-patient authorization and API): These are\nserver-to-server backend applications e.g. Export clinical data for multiple patients in\na group. This app is system level app without any UI.\n\n5.​Types of Authentications Supported\n●​ Symmetric (“client secret”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Symmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Asymmetric (“private key JWT”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Asymmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Public Clients (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for\nPublic Client - FHIR v4.0.1)\n\n6.​Steps for Smart App Launch\nClient APP Registration\nBefore a SMART app can run against an EHR, the app must be registered with that EHR’s\nauthorization service. We are using OAuth 2.0 Client Registration.\n\nClient App Request process\nThird-party application must sign the API Subscription Agreement with following details\n●​ The app name\n●​ The necessary APIs / Scopes\n●​ Any redirect URIs\n●​ Launch URL (optional)\n7\n\n\f●​ URL to JWK Set (Only for apps supporting asymmetric client authentication).\nThe application is created by the EHR admin after confirming the app’s registration\nparameters and communicates a client_id to the app.\n●​ FHIR APIs (of any supported version) listed within the USCDI v3 core data set will be\nsupported\n●​ Only reads data from FHIR server\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\nIn order to obtain launch context and request authorization to access FHIR resources, the\napp discovers the EHR FHIR server’s SMART configuration metadata, including OAuth\nauthorization_endpoint and token_endpoint URLs.\nFHIR server makes SMART configuration available from well-known endpoint. You can get\nAuthorization end point and token endpoint.\nRequest\nGET\n\n<FHIRBaseUrl>/fhir/.well-known/smart-configuration\n\nUnset\n\n{\n\n\"authorization_endpoint\":\"<AuthServerURL>/oauth2/authorize\",\n\"token_endpoint\":\"<AuthServerURL>/oauth2/token\",\n\"introspection_endpoint\":\"<AuthServerURL>/oauth2/introspect\",\n\"revocation_endpoint\":\"<AuthServerURL>/oauth2/revoke\",\n\"capabilities\":[\n\"launch-ehr\",\n\"launch-standalone\",\n\"client-public\",\n\"client-confidential-asymmetric\",\n\"client-confidential-symmetric\",\n\"sso-openid-connect\",\n\"context-banner\",\n\"context-style\",\n\"context-ehr-patient\",\n\"context-ehr-encounter\",\n\"context-standalone-patient\",\n\"context-standalone-encounter\",\n\"permission-offline\",\n\n8\n\n\f\"permission-patient\",\n\"permission-user\",\n\"permission-v1\",\n\"permission-v2\",\n\"authorize-post\"\n],\n\"grant_types_supported\":[\n\"client_credentials\",\n\"authorization_code\",\n\"refresh_token\"\n],\n\"code_challenge_methods_supported\":[\n\"S256\"\n],\n\"issuer\":\"<AuthServerURL>\",\n\"jwks_uri\":\"<AuthServerURL>/oauth2/jwks\",\n\"token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported\": [\n\"client_secret_basic\",\n\"private_key_jwt\"\n],\n\"token_endpoint_auth_signing_alg_values_supported\": [\n\"RS384\",\n\"ES384\"\n],\n\"scopes_supported\": [\n\"openid\",\n\"profile\",\n\"launch\",\n\"launch/patient\",\n\"patient/*.*\",\n\"user/*.*\",\n\"system/*.rs\",\n\"fhirUser\",\n\"offline_access\"\n],\n\"response_types_supported\": [\n\"code\",\n\"code id_token\",\n\"id_token\",\n\"refresh_token\"\n]\n}\n\nResponse Codes:\n200\n\nOk\n\n401\n\nUnauthorized\n\n9\n\n\f400\n\nBad Request\n\n500\n\nInternal Server Error\n\nObtain authorization code\nThe app supplies the following parameters to the EHR’s “authorize” endpoint.\n\nParameters\nresponse_type\n\nrequired\n\nFixed value: code\n\nclient_id\n\nrequired\n\nThe client`s identifier.\n\nredirect_uri\n\nrequired\n\nMust match one of the client's pre-registered redirect\nURIs.\n\nlaunch\n\nconditional\n\nWhen using the EHR Launch flow, this must match the\nlaunch value received from the EHR. Omitted when using\nthe Standalone\nLaunch.\n\nscope\n\nrequired\n\nMust describe the access that the app needs, including\nscopes like patient/*.rs, openid and fhirUser (if\napp needs authenticated patient identity) and either:\n●​ a launch value indicating that the app wants to\nreceive already-established launch context details\nfrom the EHR\n●​ a set of launch context requirements in the form\nlaunch/patient, which asks the EHR to\nestablish context on your behalf.\nPlease refer to the scopes supported in the table below\n\nstate\n\nrequired\n\nAn opaque value used by the client to maintain state\nbetween the request and callback. The authorization\nserver includes this value when redirecting the user-agent\nback to the client. The parameter SHALL be used for\npreventing cross-site request forgery or session fixation\nattacks. The app SHALL use an unpredictable value for\nthe state parameter with at least 122 bits of entropy (e.g.,\na properly configured random uuid is suitable).\n\naud\n\nrequired\n\nURL of the EHR resource server from which the app\nwishes to retrieve FHIR data. This parameter prevents\nleaking a genuine bearer token to a counterfeit resource\n\n10\n\n\fserver. (Note that in the case of an EHR launch flow, this\naud value is the same as the launch's iss value.) Note\nthat the aud parameter is semantically equivalent to the\nresource parameter defined in RFC8707.\nSMART's aud parameter predates RFC8707 and we\nhave decided not to rename it for reasons of backwards\ncompatibility. We might consider renaming SMART's aud\nparameter in the future if implementer feedback indicates\nthat alignment would be valuable. For the current release,\nservers SHALL support the aud parameter and MAY\nsupport a resource parameter as a synonym for aud.\n\nScopes Supported\npatient/*.r\npatient/*.read\n\nPermission to read and search any resource for the current patient (see\nnotes on wildcard scopes below).\n\nuser/*.r\nuser/*.read\n\nPermission to read and write all resources that the current user can access\n(see notes on wildcard scopes below).\n\nopenid\n\nPermission to retrieve information about the current logged-in user.\nScope Grants\n\nfhirUser\nlaunch\n\nPermission to obtain launch context when app is launched from an EHR.\n\nlaunch/patient\n\nWhen launching outside the EHR, ask for a patient to be selected at launch\ntime.\n\noffline_access\n\nRequest a refresh_token that can be used to obtain a new access token to\nreplace an expired one, even after the end-user no longer is\nonline after the access token expires.\n\nHere's an example of an authorization request using HTTP GET. You will replace\nthe [redirect_uri], [client_id], [launch_token],[scopes],[state],\n[code_challenge], and [audience] placeholders with your own values.\nRequest\n\nUnset\n\nhttps://<AuthServe​\nrURL>/oauth2/authorize?\nresponse_type=code&\nclient_id=[client_id]&\nredirect_uri=[redirect_uri]&\nlaunch=[launch_token]&\n\n11\n\n\fscope=[scopes]&\nstate=[state]&\naud=[audience]\n\nFor example:\n\nUnset\n\nhttps://[FHIRBaseURL]/oauth2/authorize?response_type=code&client_id=S\nAMPLE_CONFIDENTIAL_CLIENT_\nID&redirect_uri=https%3A%2F%2Finferno.healthit.gov%2Finferno%2Foauth2\n%2Fstatic%2Fredirect&scope=l\naunch%2Fpatient+openid+fhirUser+offline_access+patient%2FMedication.r\nead+patient%2FAllergyIntoleran\nce.read+patient%2FCarePlan.read+patient%2FCareTeam.read+patient%2FCon\ndition.read+patient%2FDevic\ne.read+patient%2FDiagnosticReport.read+patient%2FDocumentReference.re\nad+patient%2FEncounter.read\n+patient%2FGoal.read+patient%2FImmunization.read+patient%2FLocation.r\nead+patient%2FMedicationRe\nquest.read+patient%2FObservation.read+patient%2FOrganization.read+pat\nient%2FPatient.read+patient%\n2FPractitioner.read+patient%2FProcedure.read+patient%2FProvenance.rea\nd+patient%2FPractitionerRole.r\nead&state=26a45e37-7445-4e4a-b8fb24144eccbdc4&aud=https%3A%2F%2Finfer\nno.healthit.gov%2Freference-server%2Fr4\n\nResponse\nThe EHR's authorization server reviews the request from your application. If approved, the\nauthorization server redirects the browser to the redirect URL supplied in the initial request\nand appends the following querystring parameter.\n●​ code: This parameter contains the authorization code generated by EHR, which will\nbe exchanged for the access token in the next step.\n●​ state: The exact value received from the client.\nHere is how redirect URL will look like\n●​ Location: https://app/after-auth?code=123abc&state=98wrghuwuogerg97\n\n12\n\n\fUnset\n\nhttps://inferno.healthit.gov/inferno/oauth2/static/redirect?code=SAMP\nLE_CODE.bGF1bmNoL3BhdGllbnQgb3BlbmlkIGZoaXJVc2VyIG9mZmxpb\nmVfYWNjZXNzIHBhdGllbnQvTWVkaWNhdGlvbi5yZWFkIHBhdGllbnQvQWxsZXJneUludG\n9sZXJhbmNlLnJlYWQgcGF0aWVudC9DYXJlUGxhbi5yZWF\nkIHBhdGllbnQvQ2FyZVRlYW0ucmVhZCBwYXRpZW50L0NvbmRpdGlvbi5yZWFkIHBhdGll\nbnQvRGV2aWNlLnJlYWQgcGF0aWVudC9EaWFnbm9zdG\nljUmVwb3J0LnJlYWQgcGF0aWVudC9Eb2N1bWVudFJlZmVyZW5jZS5yZWFkIHBhdGllbnQ\nvRW5jb3VudGVyLnJlYWQgcGF0aWVudC9Hb2FsLnJlYW\nQgcGF0aWVudC9JbW11bml6YXRpb24ucmVhZCBwYXRpZW50L0xvY2F0aW9uLnJlYWQgcGF\n0aWVudC9NZWRpY2F0aW9uUmVxdWVzdC5yZWFkI\nHBhdGllbnQvT2JzZXJ2YXRpb24ucmVhZCBwYXRpZW50L09yZ2FuaXphdGlvbi5yZWFkIH\nBhdGllbnQvUGF0aWVudC5yZWFkIHBhdGllbnQvUHJhY3R\npdGlvbmVyLnJlYWQgcGF0aWVudC9Qcm9jZWR1cmUucmVhZCBwYXRpZW50L1Byb3ZlbmFu\nY2UucmVhZCBwYXRpZW50L1ByYWN0aXRpb25lclJv\nbGUucmVhZCA=.ODU=&state=26a45e37-7445-4e4a-b8fb-24144eccbdc4\n\nAfter receiving the authorization code, your application trades the code for a JSON object\ncontaining an access token and contextual information by sending an HTTP POST to the\ntoken endpoint using a Content-Type header with value of\n\"application/x-www-form-urlencoded\".\n\nResponse Codes: JSON response is returned from the FHIR Server along with the code\n200\n\nOk\n\n401\n\nUnauthorized\n\n400\n\nBad Request\n\n500\n\nInternal Server Error\n\nObtain access token\nFor public apps, authentication is not required because a client with no secret cannot prove\nits identity when it issues a call. (The end-to-end system can still be secure because the\nclient comes from a known, https protected endpoint specified and enforced by the redirect\nuri.)\nFor confidential apps, authentication is required. Confidential clients SHOULD use\nAsymmetric Authentication if available, and MAY use Symmetric Authentication.\n\n13\n\n\fThere are two different requests to get access token based on authentication type. In case of\nSymmetric (“client secret”) authentication app issues an HTTP POST to the EHR\nauthorization server’s token endpoint URL using content-type\napplication/x-www-form-urlencoded.\nIn case of Asymmetric authentication app, generate a client authentication assertion and\nprepare arguments for POST to token API:\nRequest\nParameters\ngrant_type\n\nrequired\n\nFixed value: authorization_code for symmetric and\nclient_credentials for asymmetric\n\ncode\n\nrequired\n\nCode that the app received from the authorization server\n\nredirect_uri\n\nrequired\n\nThe same redirect_uri used in the initial authorization\nrequest\n\nclient_id\n\nconditional\n\nRequired for public apps. Omit for confidential apps.\n\nclient_assertion_t\nype\n\nconditional\n\nRequired for asymmetric authentication. set is intended to be used by developers of apps that need to access user identity\ninformation or other FHIR resources by requesting authorization from OAuth 2.0 compliant\nauthorization servers.\nThe profile defines a method through which an app requests authorization to access a FHIR\nresource, and then uses that authorization to retrieve the resource. Synchronization of\npatient context is not addressed; for use cases that require context synchronization (e.g.,\nlearning about when the in context patient changes within an EHR session) In other words, if\nthe patient chart is changed during the session, the application will not inherently be\nupdated.\n\nSecurity and Privacy Considerations\nApp Protection\nThe app is responsible for protecting itself from potential misbehaving or malicious values\npassed to its redirect URL (e.g., values injected with executable code, such as SQL) and for\nprotecting authorization codes, access tokens, and refresh tokens from unauthorized access\nand use. The app developer must be aware of potential threats, such as malicious apps\nrunning on the same platform, counterfeit authorization servers, and counterfeit resource\nservers, and implement countermeasures to help protect both the app itself and any\nsensitive information it may hold. For background, see the OAuth 2.0 Threat Model and\nSecurity Considerations.\nSpecific requirements are:\n●​ Apps SHALL ensure that sensitive information (authentication secrets, authorization\ncodes, tokens) is transmitted ONLY to authenticated servers, over TLS-secured\nchannels.\n●​ Apps SHALL generate an unpredictable state parameter for each user session;\nSHALL include state with all authorization requests; and SHALL validate the state\nvalue for any request sent to its redirect URL.\n●​ An app SHALL NOT execute untrusted user-supplied inputs as code.\n●​ App SHALL NOT forward values passed back to its redirect URL to any other\narbitrary or userprovided URL (a practice known as an “open redirector”).\n\n70\n\n\f●​ An app SHALL NOT store bearer tokens in cookies that are transmitted as clear text.\n●​ Apps SHOULD persist tokens and other sensitive data in app-specific storage\nlocations only, and SHOULD NOT persist them in system-wide-discoverable\nlocations.\nTerms of Use\n1. The API makes health information broadly\navailable using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the\nHL7 standards organization. FHIR is based on common web standards that can be reached\nthrough a RESTful protocol in which each FHIR resource has a known URL. This document\ndescribes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with\nFHIR-based data systems.\nWe follow Smart-On-FHIR authentication (OAuth2) and authorization (Ref.\nhttp://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/toc.html )\n\n3.​FHIR Endpoints\nNote: Production and Test Server endpoints are subject to change.\n\nProduction Endpoint\nFHIR Base Url :\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.production.awscarbonhealth.com/hapi-fhir\n\nAuthentication Server Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.production.awscarbonhealth.com/fhir-smart-auth\n\nTest Server Endpoint\nFHIR Base Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.alpha.awscarbonhealth.com/hapi-fhir\n\nAuthentication Server Url:\n-​\n\nhttps://api-gateway.alpha.awscarbonhealth.com/fhir-smart-auth\n\n6\n\n\f4.​Types of Smart-On-FHIR Applications\n●​ Standalone App: SMART on FHIR confidential client with a patient context, refresh\ntoken, and OpenID Connect (OIDC) identity token.\n●​ EHR Embedded app: Demonstrate the ability to perform an EHR launch to a\nSMART on FHIR\n●​ Smart Backend Services App (Multi-patient authorization and API): These are\nserver-to-server backend applications e.g. Export clinical data for multiple patients in\na group. This app is system level app without any UI.\n\n5.​Types of Authentications Supported\n●​ Symmetric (“client secret”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Symmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Asymmetric (“private key JWT”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Asymmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Public Clients (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for\nPublic Client - FHIR v4.0.1)\n\n6.​Steps for Smart App Launch\nClient APP Registration\nBefore a SMART app can run against an EHR, the app must be registered with that EHR’s\nauthorization service. We are using OAuth 2.0 Client Registration.\n\nClient App Request process\nThird-party application must sign the API Subscription Agreement with following details\n●​ The app name\n●​ The necessary APIs / Scopes\n●​ Any redirect URIs\n●​ Launch URL (optional)\n7\n\n\f●​ URL to JWK Set (Only for apps supporting asymmetric client authentication).\nThe application is created by the EHR admin after confirming the app’s registration\nparameters and communicates a client_id to the app.\n●​ FHIR APIs (of any supported version) listed within the USCDI v3 core data set will be\nsupported\n●​ Only reads data from FHIR server\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\nIn order to obtain launch context and request authorization to access FHIR resources, the\napp discovers the EHR FHIR server’s SMART configuration metadata, including OAuth\nauthorization_endpoint and token_endpoint URLs.\nFHIR server makes SMART configuration available from well-known endpoint. FHIR is based on common web standards that can be reached\nthrough a RESTful protocol in which each FHIR resource has a known URL. This app is system level app without any UI.\n\n5.​Types of Authentications Supported\n●​ Symmetric (“client secret”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Symmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Asymmetric (“private key JWT”) authentication\n(HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Asymmetric Client\nAuth - FHIR v4.0.1)\n●​ Public Clients (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for\nPublic Client - FHIR v4.0.1)\n\n6.​Steps for Smart App Launch\nClient APP Registration\nBefore a SMART app can run against an EHR, the app must be registered with that EHR’s\nauthorization service. We are using OAuth 2.0 Client Registration.\n\nClient App Request process\nThird-party application must sign the API Subscription Agreement with following details\n●​ The app name\n●​ The necessary APIs / Scopes\n●​ Any redirect URIs\n●​ Launch URL (optional)\n7\n\n\f●​ URL to JWK Set (Only for apps supporting asymmetric client authentication).\nThe application is created by the EHR admin after confirming the app’s registration\nparameters and communicates a client_id to the app.\n●​ FHIR APIs (of any supported version) listed within the USCDI v3 core data set will be\nsupported\n●​ Only reads data from FHIR server\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\nIn order to obtain launch context and request authorization to access FHIR resources, the\napp discovers the EHR FHIR server’s SMART configuration metadata, including OAuth\nauthorization_endpoint and token_endpoint URLs.\nFHIR server makes SMART configuration available from well-known endpoint. Here is the complete substance:\n\n**Export mechanism**: CarbyOs EHR supports both single-patient and patient-population (bulk) EHI exports.\n\n**Export formats**:\n- **C-CDA documents** for clinical records\n- **PDFs** for billing and claims information\n- **Original native formats** for uploaded documents\n\n**Exclusions**: Psychotherapy notes (per 45 CFR 164.501) and information compiled for legal proceedings.\n\n**Access control**: System administrators manage the ability to perform EHI exports.\n\n**No other EHI export documentation exists.** There is no data dictionary, no field-level documentation, no schema, no sample exports, no instructions for how to perform the export, and no description of what specific data elements are included in the C-CDA or billing PDFs.\n\n### FHIR API PDF (carbonhealth-fhir-api-doc-v1_2.pdf)\n\nThis is a 74-page document for the **170.315(g)(10) SMART-on-FHIR API** — not the (b)(10) EHI export. It documents:\n- SMART on FHIR app launch flows (standalone, EHR-embedded, backend services)\n- OAuth 2.0 authentication (symmetric, asymmetric, public client)\n- FHIR R4 endpoints and USCDI v3 data class mappings to US Core 6.1.0 profiles\n- Supported FHIR resources: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, Coverage, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, Goal, Immunization, Location, MedicationDispense, MedicationRequest, Observation, Procedure, ServiceRequest, Organization, Practitioner, Provenance, RelatedPerson, Specimen\n\nThis document is relevant for understanding what clinical data the product can expose via FHIR, but it explicitly covers only the USCDI v3 subset — not the full EHI designated record set.\n\n### Safety Enhanced Design PDF (sed-testing.pdf)\n\nA 36-page NISTIR 7742 usability test report from August 2025. This documents usability testing of the CarbyOs EHR interface. Not relevant to EHI export data content, but included for completeness as it was on the certification disclosures page.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe product research identifies CarbyOs as storing extensive clinical, billing, operational, and patient engagement data. The EHI export description makes only the following claims:\n\n| Data Domain | Covered? | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Clinical records (notes, problems, meds, labs, vitals, allergies) | Claimed via \"C-CDA documents\" | No specifics — which C-CDA sections? What templates? |\n| Billing and claims | Claimed via \"PDFs\" | No detail on what billing data is included. PDFs are not computable. |\n| Uploaded documents | Claimed via \"original native formats\" | Reasonable approach for uploaded artifacts |\n| E-prescriptions | Unknown | Not mentioned separately; may be in C-CDA |\n| Lab orders/results | Unknown | May be in C-CDA, not specified |\n| Imaging orders/references | Unknown | May be in C-CDA, not specified |\n| Referral packages | Unknown | Not mentioned |\n| Care plans | Unknown | May be in C-CDA, not specified |\n| Patient engagement data (messages, scheduling) | Unknown | Not mentioned |\n| Insurance/eligibility information | Unknown | Possibly in billing PDFs, not specified |\n| AI-generated charting data (SOAP notes from ambient AI) | Unknown | Not mentioned — significant gap given this is the product's flagship feature |\n| Claim scrubbing/correction history | Unknown | Not mentioned |\n| Payment records | Unknown | Possibly in billing PDFs, not specified |\n\n**Critical observation**: The export description says \"C-CDA documents for clinical records\" but provides zero detail about which C-CDA document types, templates, or sections are included. A C-CDA could range from a minimal CCD (essentially a USCDI summary) to a comprehensive export with custom sections. Without a data dictionary or template documentation, it's impossible to assess actual coverage.\n\n**Billing as PDF**: Exporting billing data as PDFs is problematic for (b)(10) compliance. PDFs are technically \"electronic\" but barely \"computable.\" A third party receiving billing PDFs would need to OCR or manually extract data. The regulation requires export in an \"electronic and computable\" format. Structured billing data (claims, charges, payments) would be better served by CSV, 837, or JSON exports.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Clinical data**: C-CDA (a recognized standard, but scope unknown)\n- **Billing data**: PDF (not computable — a significant weakness)\n- **Uploaded documents**: Original native formats (reasonable)\n\nThe use of C-CDA for clinical data is appropriate if implemented comprehensively. However, without documentation of which C-CDA templates and sections are used, we cannot assess whether this covers only a USCDI summary (essentially the same as g(10) output in document form) or the full clinical record.\n\nThe FHIR API documentation covers the same USCDI v3 data classes that would be in a standard CCD/CCD-A. If the (b)(10) C-CDA export is just a CCD generated from the same data that feeds the (g)(10) API, then the (b)(10) export adds no additional coverage beyond what's already available via the standardized API — it would be a (g)(10) equivalent in C-CDA clothing, not a true \"all EHI\" export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Extremely poor.** The EHI export documentation consists of approximately 150 words of marketing-quality prose on the certification disclosures page. There is:\n\n- **No data dictionary** — no listing of tables, fields, data types, or relationships\n- **No schema documentation** — no C-CDA templates specified, no XSD references\n- **No sample exports** — no example files showing what the output looks like\n- **No export instructions** — no screenshots, no step-by-step guide for performing an export\n- **No format specification** — \"C-CDA documents\" and \"PDFs\" is the entire format description\n- **No field-level documentation** — impossible to know what's included or excluded\n- **No value set documentation** — no coded field definitions\n- **No relationship documentation** — no description of how clinical and billing data link together\n\nA developer tasked with importing this data would have essentially no information to work with beyond \"expect some C-CDA files and some PDFs.\"\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe documentation is effectively a compliance checkbox statement — it asserts that the export exists and lists three format categories, but provides no technical substance. This is among the least detailed EHI export documentation possible while still technically making a claim about (b)(10) compliance.\n\n**Key gaps**:\n1. **No data dictionary at all** — this is the most fundamental gap. Without knowing what data elements are exported, the documentation is effectively empty.\n2. **Billing PDFs are not computable** — this likely fails the \"electronic and computable\" requirement of (b)(10).\n3. **No mention of specialty-specific data** — CarbyOs is used in urgent care and primary care. While these are relatively standard clinical settings, the product's AI-generated charting data (a core differentiator) is not discussed in the export context.\n4. **No distinction from (g)(10)** — there's no indication that the (b)(10) export covers anything beyond what's already available through the FHIR API, except for billing PDFs and uploaded documents.\n5. **No bulk export format documentation** — the population-level export is mentioned but not described technically. Is it a ZIP of individual C-CDAs? A different format?\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nCarbon Health's EHI export documentation is minimal and non-technical. The certification disclosures page makes the required (b)(10) compliance assertion but provides no supporting technical documentation. The only downloadable technical document (the FHIR API PDF) covers the (g)(10) standardized API, not the (b)(10) EHI export.\n\nThis is compounded by the company's February 2026 Chapter 11 bankruptcy filing. While operations reportedly continue, the sparse documentation may reflect limited investment in compliance documentation infrastructure. The version 2 product (CHPL 11590, certified January 2025) was certified only for (b)(10) and infrastructure criteria, suggesting minimal initial certification. Version 3 (CHPL 11700, certified September 2025) added comprehensive clinical and API certifications, but the EHI export documentation did not appear to be enhanced.\n\nThe export approach itself (C-CDA for clinical + PDF for billing + native for uploads) is reasonable in concept, but the total absence of technical documentation makes it impossible to verify actual coverage or quality. The billing-as-PDF approach is a specific concern for computability.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ehr-support.carbyos.com/certification-disclosures\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, no JavaScript needed for content)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI section on the single page, linked via anchor #ehi-export-functionality)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- No obstacles accessing the page or downloading files.\n- FHIR production endpoint (`api-gateway.production.awscarbonhealth.com`) returned empty responses (requires authentication) — no public CapabilityStatement available.\n- No additional EHI documentation was found anywhere on the ehr-support.carbyos.com site, the carbyos.com main site, or linked from the certification disclosures page.\n- The SED PDF and FHIR API PDF contained no links to additional EHI export documentation.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":449,"cache_read_input_tokens":57361,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":449},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f67b441f-d8f3-4dc1-abd3-d0d625138a5b","uuid":"a7fa7498-609d-48dc-9aae-9f7d0cb0b3df"}
